## Opaque continuation retention and edit policy

**User decision, 2026-11-02 — MOD-01.01b / MOD-01.04b:** `fais comme pi` selects the remaining retention/edit behavior from Pi 1.2.0, commit `06a466935536f8c9bcacb2d5efc0fc021be1bdff`. The earlier explicit [JSON-export choice](#opaque-continuation-json-export-policy) remains unchanged: expurgated by default, warning, complete option only on explicit request, and API keys excluded in both modes. This is a selected reference contract, not an implemented or runtime-verified carrier.

| Boundary | Pinned behavior to follow |
|---|---|
| Stored content | Pi's [content types](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/06a466935536f8c9bcacb2d5efc0fc021be1bdff/packages/ai/src/types.ts#L413-L444) attach opaque `textSignature`, `thinkingSignature`/`redacted`, and `thoughtSignature` to their respective blocks. [Session persistence](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/06a466935536f8c9bcacb2d5efc0fc021be1bdff/packages/coding-agent/src/core/session-manager.ts#L1172-L1215) serializes message entries without stripping those fields; no-session remains memory-only. Follow normal journal/branch lifecycle, not an invented signature TTL or a ban on user-requested fork copies. |
| Editing and context | [Context projection](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/06a466935536f8c9bcacb2d5efc0fc021be1bdff/packages/coding-agent/src/core/session-manager.ts#L508-L588) applies branch-local omission/content replacement without rewriting the raw target or globally invalidating later signatures. [Re-editing a user message](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/06a466935536f8c9bcacb2d5efc0fc021be1bdff/packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts#L4114-L4162) branches from its parent; abandoned entries remain history. Context omission is not secure erasure. |
| Request replay | The [SDK transformer](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/06a466935536f8c9bcacb2d5efc0fc021be1bdff/packages/ai/src/api/transform-messages.ts#L95-L149) distinguishes exact provider/API/model identity: same-identity signed blocks survive; cross-identity encrypted/redacted thinking is omitted, visible thinking becomes text, and tool thought signatures are removed. Native owner rules still apply: [Bedrock Claude missing-signature reasoning falls back to text](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/06a466935536f8c9bcacb2d5efc0fc021be1bdff/packages/ai/src/api/bedrock-converse-stream.ts#L1036-L1077); malformed encrypted payloads are omitted by its decoder, not a blanket whole-request refusal. |

The assistant's earlier suggested blanket suffix invalidation and mandatory refusal are **not** the selected Pi policy. Provider-redacted encrypted thinking is replay data, not permission to disable AiHarness credential redaction. Existing secret protection stays mandatory; its carrier interaction still needs implementation and focused verification. Do not copy Pi's session-v3 format into AiHarness v2 or infer cryptographic/service authenticity from opaque strings.

This bounded static review uses a clean pinned checkout and the named owning functions; it is not a fresh SDK capture, execution of upstream tests or review of the full historical corpus. The concrete Core carrier/export implementation remains outstanding, with SDK + agent + persistence scope retained. No runtime integration, criterion closure, accepted gap, diagnostic expansion or QUA-01 restart follows. See the [delivery board](./MOD-01-tracking.md).

## Opaque continuation JSON export policy

**User decision, 2026-11-02 — MOD-01.01b / MOD-01.04b; selected, not implemented or runtime-verified.** In this conversation, the user explicitly selected: “l’export expurgé par défaut, avec avertissement et option complète explicite.”

- **Default JSON session export:** omit opaque continuation payloads, retain the visible conversation, and clearly warn that native continuation may no longer be possible after import.
- **Explicit complete option:** include the opaque continuation data only when the user explicitly requests the complete export. This preserves available continuation data, not a guarantee of provider/account compatibility or successful replay.
- **Both modes:** exclude API keys. A complete export is not a credential dump and must not bypass credential protection.

This resolves the JSON-export policy choice only. SDK + agent + persistence remains the selected scope. Internal retention/deletion rules, the concrete carrier/public surface, and redaction/edit/compaction invalidation policy are not selected by this reply. Earlier export-policy-pending wording is historical; the older carrier proposal is still not an approved mechanism. No CLI flag, SDK/API option or export implementation is delivered by this documentary record. The QUA-01 stop, preserved failures, open criteria and all other pending user decisions remain unchanged; no diagnostic or qualification campaign is restarted. See the [delivery board](./MOD-01-tracking.md).

## MOD-01.03a — Native Mistral CLI composition (2026-11-01)

**Implemented and selectively integrated as `bd05889`; required parity/root qualification remains failed.** [Durable source-bound receipt](./evidence/MOD-01.03a-native-mistral-cli.json). Candidate `bb844a05770e1c3d9c4609645a3b5f77c559b17d`, tree selected from clean `470bfbf`, excludes concurrent Anthropic and documentary changes. No GDB/collector work is resumed. This delivers one existing 03a host-composition requirement, not Mistral OAuth, Server/Web builtin admission, complete effective profiles or the other SDK-only adapters.

The missing behavior was native environment/command/encrypted credentials and startup settings/scopes **without an extension**. Existing explicit Mistral process scenarios now also exercise native print/JSON/RPC, while preserving bare `mistral` and arbitrary operator registrations. The existing installed Mistral consumer retains its SDK/declaration assertions and adds the three genuine PATH-bin modes. Existing credential fixtures are parameterized for native Mistral, adding offline selected-command refusal without falling through to the stored key. No assertions, deadlines, runtime flags, dependencies, baselines or public DTO shapes are weakened.

Selection/settings/storage use `mistral-conversations`. Environment order is `AI_HARNESS_MISTRAL_CONVERSATIONS_API_KEY`, `MISTRAL_CONVERSATIONS_API_KEY`, `MISTRAL_API_KEY`; trusted commands, memory and encrypted storage follow existing shared precedence. `MISTRAL_MODEL` defaults to `mistral-small-latest`; `MISTRAL_BASE_URL` is optional. No stored identity is migrated. Native composition inherits existing network/redaction and passive offline policy.

Docker results on this exact candidate: canonical `make typecheck` **passes**; four focused files **40/40 pass**; selected genuine installed consumer **1 pass / 332 unselected**, including public declarations/SDK and three native CLI prompts. `make test-parity` **fails: 366 pass / two fail / 368 total**. Both failures occur in TypeScript consumers before runtime probes: OpenCode Go compiler `SIGSEGV`, Xiaomi compiler `SIGTRAP` with a native fatal diagnostic. Mistral passes within that failed gate. Cause is unknown; compiler termination alone demonstrates no application regression, but the required parity gate is not satisfied. No retry, engine/debugger investigation or historical repair is credited. The planned independent `make test` also **fails: 8,539 pass / one fail / 8,540 total across 525 files**; the Baseten public TypeScript compiler receives `SIGSEGV` before its runtime consumer. Cause remains unknown; no application regression is demonstrated solely by that compiler exit. All Mistral scenarios pass within the root. No unchanged retry or mitigation is launched.

All three canonical target image names resolve to the same image ID `sha256:7fc0371cd90e7a20a08c72db2e2cb032ad16d7d2b7141079b66dc4c641f74a9c`. The frozen Git tree owns all 1,690 tracked inputs; 1,544 included paths match image bytes/modes, 146 documented/excluded paths are absent and zero differ. This is a canonical **cached dependency build**, not a fresh dependency installation. Existing offline installed fixtures explicitly seed installed dependencies; they are not registry/distribution acceptance. Before integration, all 1,544 Docker-included staged blobs/modes were checked against that qualified candidate; only excluded documentation/evidence was additionally staged. Concurrent Anthropic code, fixture, installed hunks and documentary cleanup were not incorporated. Final integrated gates, production/install/platform/visual acceptance and all seven criterion statuses remain open. The receipt embeds all 18 raw source/image/log/exit artifacts compressed with byte hashes; selective integration does not convert either failed required gate into a success.

## Scope and acceptance

The requested outcome is usable provider/model integrations, including the approved API-key, OAuth and subscription methods, not a list of names. The reference inventory has 42 factory identities and 1,649 API/type entries; these are not 1,649 distinct usable models.

On 2026-10-26 the user authorized integration, validation and commits of existing MOD-01 changes, excluding unrelated changes. The user also confirmed that real accounts are unavailable: execute local acceptance without them. This removes live-account testing from this execution's gates; it does **not** waive missing authentication implementations or certify vendor access, entitlements, current availability, billing, regional TLS or service interoperability. Keep that limit in the final receipt.

Use disposable credentials and loopback/intercepted transports. OAuth controls must use an owned HTTPS issuer with a test CA, not disabled certificate verification. Catalogue reads and offline startup must not trigger credential commands, login, refresh or discovery. Record unknown costs as unknown, never free.

## Delivery order

The register below preserves all 23 requirements and their acceptance boundaries; it is not a mandate to restart them in numerical order. Each task requires implementation, focused regression evidence, documentation and a bounded commit. Preserve old failed receipts; a new successful gate does not explain or erase them. Reuse existing Core/provider, emitted CLI, installed-package, HTTP and browser families rather than adding another test framework.

## Opus 4.6 signed carrier proposal (HOLD)

**Carrier approved by the user — 2026-11-07:** “pour Bedrock : j'autorise l’utilisation de ChatResponse.blocks comme transport signé SDK”. Use the existing public `ChatResponse.blocks` carrier for the Bedrock signed SDK path, starting with Opus4.6, and preserve its agent/durable reload/exact-physical-owner replay protections. This explicit decision lifts the carrier-choice HOLD; it is not implementation, test acceptance, signature authentication, expanded model admission or permission to bypass redaction/publication guards. This is the bounded design follow-up for MOD-01.01b / MOD-01.04b, coordinated with MOD-03 before implementation. Static source is the clean isolated `ac6b6fa4f88bf44e1eb5006f585102cf5059c3c6` / tree `7a485087d0a0d37d76eb8eb25737d5672b3d5cc4`, not the dirty main provider/session files. ### Observed handoff boundaries

| Source at the isolated head | Static finding / design consequence |
|---|---|
| `providers/index.ts:281–306,4299–4340,4398–4452` under `packages/core/src/` | Public `ChatResponse` has reasoning text only. Bedrock JSON chat extracts text/tools; request lowering emits text/images/tools, not signed reasoning. Adding a field to that exported interface would be a public contract change, not an internal-only fix. |
| `providers/bedrock-converse-stream.ts:150–197` | Stream parsing aggregates visible reasoning text; signature/redacted data is not returned. It does not retain the ordered native block vector needed for E13 replay. Any initial repair must stay behind the exact native Opus4.6 owner, not silently change the other Bedrock profiles. |
| `agent/provider-turn.ts:283–325,520–564` and `agent/tool-loop.ts:211–299` | Extensible redaction, response spreads, usage normalization and before:message publication recreate objects. A WeakMap/nonenumerable property is not automatically transferred. Both tool-free and tool-producing append paths construct new Messages. Billable usage and existing output-veto behavior must remain separate from replay eligibility. |
| `agent/provider-request-payload.ts:67–86,164–181` | `MODEL` selects strict slot validation; it is **not** a whitelist that drops all metadata. Ordinary enumerable metadata can survive legacy copying, but is passed through a redactor and is not a validated signed carrier. Do not widen `MODEL` or promote caller metadata into dispatch/provenance authority. |
| `sessions/session-manager.ts:314–344,2030–2078` and `sessions/serialization.ts:69–109` | Message metadata is copied into entries, subjected to configured redaction, and retained by ordinary serialized Message spreads. A reserved metadata key would be observable session data, not a hidden channel. Generic JSON/JSONL round trips do not guarantee opaque-byte preservation under redaction. |
| `agent/tool-loop.ts:114–134` and `sessions/compaction-model-messages.ts:15–36` | Effective-turn and compaction projections copy metadata. An eventual carrier needs an explicit summarization/edit/switch policy; it must not leak blindly to another model or be synthesized from summary text. The existing physical projection also needs its API/identity boundary reviewed, not just its provider/model comparison. |

Bounded reading record: 16 source/test files / 765,202 bytes inventoried, with only the recorded slices semantically reviewed; all1,684 source file bytes/modes still match E13. Local `/tmp/aih-mod01-bedrock-opus46-carrier-design-r1/source-review.json` is 53,724 bytes, SHA256 `6797524e5d89dae6d152be74f7557849950f578efbdf9a6362212a8c4efc5787`. It is not a full durable raw archive or a fresh review of the historical audit. First inventory preparation failed on two slice ends one line beyond EOF; the unchanged script/manual tool-failure record and separately corrected EOF-bound record are retained. No application/test/SDK execution was added.

### Decisions required before a source/test admission

| Choice | Recommendation and unresolved consequence |
|---|---|
| Carrier and public surface | **Prefer a bounded agent-managed internal handoff plus an explicitly reserved persisted Message metadata envelope, only if its observable export/redaction behavior is approved.** Keep API1/schema2/public DTO shapes and shared `MODEL` unchanged. Carry a version, captured Core physical provider/API/model identity and an ordered vector of visible-reasoning+opaque-signature or redacted bytes plus associated public content; base64 is reversible encoding, never signature validation. Do not choose a key/schema as already normative here. |
| Standalone provider SDK | A private in-process handoff can serve a Core-managed turn, but `AiProvider.chat/streamChat` consumers cannot reconstruct a typed opaque reply through the present public `ChatResponse`. Choose whether this first slice is explicitly agent-managed only, leaving standalone provider continuation open, or authorize a separately reviewed public contract proposal. Do not claim that a hidden property or the installed request tests solve this boundary. A memory-only carrier is insufficient for durable reload. |
| Redaction and edits | Never bypass an existing redactor or repair/re-sign opaque bytes. Approve an explicit policy for changed/removed content, hooks, context edits, compaction and failed publication. Proposed conservative behavior is to withhold invalidated replay eligibility and refuse any next dispatch that would require inventing it, preserving completed-call usage; this is a product-policy proposal, **not** a Pi-equivalence verdict. Drop-versus-refuse and legitimate plain-text fallback still need bounded reference controls before acceptance. |
| Identity and history | Bind to the actual Core physical identity, not a virtual alias or blindly copied Pi `amazon-bedrock` label. Require exact model/API/provider for any admitted replay; cross-identity conversion and prefix changes need their own observed controls. Opus4.6 request `thinking.block_binding` remains excluded: E13 does not show service prefix enforcement or justify inventing a prefix-mismatch policy. |
| Bounds and publication | Keep current frame1MiB, stream-output16MiB/transport64MiB, block1024 and session-message16MiB ceilings; account for base64 growth and apply an approved bounded envelope policy within them, not raised budgets. Never publish an incomplete/aborted/synthetic replacement as a completed signed response. Parsing inert caller data cannot certify service origin. Credentials, account authority and signature verification are outside this envelope. |

### Focused future verification, not executed

Existing coverage is reusable but does not cover the new carrier: `providers/bedrock-converse-stream.test.ts:88–122` tests binary text/reasoning/tools/usage; `agent/provider-request-payload.test.ts:31–40,58–64` tests owned redaction/serializer inertness; `agent/tool-results.test.ts:32–44` preserves usage on message veto; `agent/reply-checkpoints.test.ts:122–138` preserves failed visible output; `sessions/serialization.test.ts:80–99` checks generic rich-block/date round trips. These are source observations, not newly run or transferred gates.

After the decisions, first define a bounded RED slice reusing E13's two immutable physical response sequences and actual continuation bodies for the exact managed streaming owner. Then add only the missing handoff/clone/redaction, both append paths, JSON and JSONL reload, order/byte preservation and withdrawal assertions in their owning scenarios. Malformed/partial/empty-signature, tool, identity-switch, edited-prefix, compaction and direct-provider SDK cases require distinct reference/scope decisions before they can earn credit. The JSON facade is not qualified by a binary reply alone. Retain outside-checkout installed, CLI/RPC, HTTP and browser evidence as distinct host boundaries; do not create a new test framework or replay successful old gates. Any execution needs a fresh prospective admission with its own inputs/budgets/maximumAttempts.

No implementation or new capture is admitted by this proposal. User owns carrier/export/privacy and SDK-scope decisions; agent owns a revised bounded plan after those decisions. MOD-01.01b/04b remain open, QUA-01 remains PARTIAL and no provider cell closes. The other connection-method, host, profile, catalogue/offline and final-qualification requirements in delivery groups1–6 are unchanged; canonical dependency acceptance, all137 original E2E failures, baseline decisions, runtime integration and full durable evidence remain unresolved.

### MOD-01.01b — Anthropic non-managed adaptive reasoning sub-lot (2026-10-27; IN PROGRESS)

Direct `AnthropicProvider` now owns the six pinned non-managed adaptive maps: `claude-fable-5`, `claude-opus-4-6`, `claude-opus-4-7`, `claude-opus-4-8`, `claude-sonnet-4-6` and `claude-sonnet-5`. `anthropic-profiles.ts` keeps the private maps separate from defensively copied metadata; `anthropic-thinking.ts` applies them to both JSON/SSE and captured native-output preflight, independently of the operator label or response alias. Native adaptive requests use `thinking.type: adaptive`, summarized display and mapped `output_config.effort`, without inspecting numeric budget maps. Unsupported `xhigh` clamps upward to `max` for Opus/Sonnet 4.6; the other four have native `xhigh`. Fable's explicit `off` clamps upward to `minimal`/wire `low`, never a false disabled claim. Other explicit off requests disable thinking. Enabled adaptive thinking omits temperature; Opus 4.7/4.8 omit it even when thinking is off or absent.

This is a bounded request-dialect migration, not complete Pi simple-options behavior. Omitted thinking still omits the preference; omitted output limits and existing per-level output allocation remain unchanged. Explicit valid adaptive caps remain exact, including 64 tokens, and the prior physical ceiling guards still apply. Captured configured models remain pinned while explicit per-call models select their own policy. ### MOD-01.01b — Anthropic managed effort and numeric allocation (2026-10-27; IN PROGRESS)

The current direct-owner implementation adds the five pinned managed profiles (`claude-fable-5-1`, `claude-haiku-5-5`, `claude-opus-5`, `claude-opus-5-5`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`). JSON/SSE and native preflight share private maps. Managed requests always use adaptive summarized thinking with `block_binding.prefix_mismatch_behavior: drop_block`, fixed top-level effort `high`, no temperature, and a trailing active-effort system control. Omitted preference selects high; explicit unsupported levels clamp upward through the pinned map. Both required mid-conversation/binding beta headers are merged with configured features. Historical assistant controls accept only a validated physical effort from the same provider and `anthropic-messages`; a logical selection, foreign provider/API or malformed value grants no replay authority. Controls never split a tool-use/result pair.

Physical effort is the additive optional `ChatResponse.providerThinkingLevel`, not the pre-existing logical `thinkingLevel`. Both assistant persistence paths in `packages/core/src/agent/tool-loop.ts` retain it in message metadata, and live/persisted CLI JSON retain validated values. The six 4.5 alias/date profiles now have owned numeric maps. Native defaults are minimal 1,024 / low 2,048 / medium 8,192 / high 16,384 tokens; unsupported xhigh/max clamp to high. Omitted output uses the published 64,000-token ceiling. An explicit answer limit can grow by the requested reasoning budget, capped privately at 64,000. The pinned helper reserves 1,024 answer tokens only when reasoning reaches the total ceiling; otherwise a valid requested thinking budget remains exact, even with a small explicit answer limit. This is not exact explicit-total-cap semantics. Safe custom budgets, including very large values, cannot overflow addition or exceed the physical ceiling. Zero retains the reference's effective 1,024 fallback. Effective thinking below the native 1,024 minimum or consuming the whole output ceiling is refused before HTTP; valid minimal allocations with one or 64 answer tokens are not rejected. Enabled numeric thinking uses summarized display/interleaved beta and omits temperature; off disables it. Unused maps stay opaque; consumed maps are copied without invoking getters.

All seventeen owned maps are published defensively, without new paid warming or inherited authority for compatible subclasses/unknown IDs. At this managed/numeric delivery, omitted output on the eleven adaptive/managed profiles had not been migrated to the pinned 128,000-token default; that separate increment is recorded below. |---|---|---|

| Minimal, answer 64; total 1,088 | Thinking 1,024 | Thinking 64 |

### MOD-01.01b — Owned Anthropic response/terminal controls (2026-10-27; IN PROGRESS)

The direct owner and exact effective native model now use [anthropic-response.ts](../../../packages/core/src/providers/anthropic-response.ts) from the existing JSON/SSE paths. JSON validates consumed envelope/content/tool fields and concatenates every text block; empty or thinking-only successful content is allowed. SSE includes initial text/thinking, retains initial tool input when no argument deltas follow, and requires message start, a successful finish and message stop before completion or pending tool publication. Successful finishes are `end_turn`, `stop_sequence`, `tool_use`, `max_tokens` and `pause_turn`; only `max_tokens` maps to Core's `length`. Error/unknown/missing finishes cannot complete. Compatible subclasses, unknown IDs and per-call unknown overrides retain legacy hooks/text handling. ### MOD-01.01b — user-selected Pi handling of output-truncated tool calls (implementation pending)

The user explicitly selects reproduction of pinned Pi1.2.0 behavior rather than either earlier simplified alternative (silently omitting unfinished calls or failing the whole turn). When an assistant message ends with `stopReason: "length"`, preserve its received text, reasoning and observed usage. Execute **none** of that message's tool calls, including calls whose arguments appear complete. Retain the calls and append a correlated error result for each, explaining that output-limit truncation prevented execution and asking the model to re-issue the call with complete arguments. Continue the agent loop so the model can recover; do not silently present truncation as normal completion.

Source at pinned commit `06a466935536f8c9bcacb2d5efc0fc021be1bdff`: `packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts`, the `length` branch and `failToolCallsFromTruncatedMessage`; existing reference test `should not execute tool calls from a length-truncated assistant message` in `packages/agent/test/agent-loop.test.ts` checks nonexecution, error results and continuation. This is static source/test inspection, not a newly executed reference test or proof of AiHarness behavior. Transport errors, absent terminal events and other stop reasons are not reclassified by this decision.

Remaining work is implementation and focused regression evidence across the owning provider/agent boundary: no side effects from any tool in a truncated message, preserved partial response/accounting, correlated errors and recovery on a subsequent model turn. This entry records the selected contract only; it integrates no product/test/fixture candidate, reruns no behavioral gate and repairs no historical failed receipt. MOD-01.01b remains **IN PROGRESS**; real-account/service qualification stays unavailable and unclaimed.

## MOD-01.01b — output-truncated tool-call recovery implemented and locally verified (2026-10-31)

**Functional result complete; selected implementation committed as `ab90965`.** On an explicit `stopReason: "length"` with calls, `packages/core/src/agent/tool-loop.ts` preserves assistant text/reasoning/usage and calls, executes none (including valid-looking arguments), saves a correlated error for each and continues ordinary tool rounds for complete re-issuance. Execution hooks, composite/registry dispatch, parallel classification and activation are bypassed.
